---
name: pm-gitlab
description: GitLab connector knowledge and safe action guide.
---

# pm-gitlab

## Purpose

Runs 582 source-bound direct reads, four GitLab REST ETL streams, and 381 source-bound mutations through direct-write and approval-gated reverse-ETL commands; its 1,752-row provider ledger retains a cited named-foundation outcome for every remaining source operation.

## Icon

- id: gitlab
- asset: icons/gitlab.svg
- source: upstream_registry
- review_status: upstream_seeded
- review_url: https://docs.gitlab.com/ee/api/rest/deprecations.html

## Capabilities

- check=true catalog=true read=true write=true query=false
- Integration type: api

## Authentication

- Use pm credentials add with --from-env or --value-stdin for secret fields.

## Configuration

- base_url
- mode
- page_size
- start_date
- access_token (secret) (required)

## ETL Streams

- projects:
  - primary key: id
  - cursor: last_activity_at
  - fields: archived(boolean), created_at(string), default_branch(string), description(string), forks_count(integer), id(integer), last_activity_at(string), name(string), open_issues_count(integer), path(string), path_with_namespace(string), star_count(integer), visibility(string), web_url(string)
- groups:
  - primary key: id
  - cursor: created_at
  - fields: created_at(string), description(string), full_name(string), full_path(string), id(integer), name(string), parent_id(integer), path(string), visibility(string), web_url(string)
- users:
  - primary key: id
  - cursor: created_at
  - fields: bot(boolean), created_at(string), id(integer), is_admin(boolean), name(string), state(string), username(string), web_url(string)
- issues:
  - primary key: id
  - cursor: updated_at
  - fields: author_id(integer), closed_at(string), created_at(string), downvotes(integer), id(integer), iid(integer), project_id(integer), state(string), title(string), updated_at(string), upvotes(integer), user_notes_count(integer), web_url(string)

## Sync Modes

- ETL sync modes: full_refresh_append, full_refresh_overwrite, full_refresh_overwrite_deduped

  - endpoint: PUT /user/{{ record.user_id }}/credit_card_validation
  - required fields: user_id, credit_card_expiration_month, credit_card_expiration_year, credit_card_holder_name, credit_card_mask_number, credit_card_type, credit_card_validated_at
  - optional fields: stripe_card_fingerprint, stripe_payment_method_xid, stripe_setup_intent_xid, zuora_payment_method_xid
  - risk: source-bound mutation; requires reverse ETL plan -> preview -> explicit approval -> execute and typed confirmation

## Security

- read risk: external GitLab API read of projects, groups, users, and issues
- write risk: 381 source-bound GitLab mutations have one-request direct-write commands and approval-gated reverse-ETL commands; the remaining provider mutations retain cited named-foundation outcomes.
- approval: Every GitLab reverse-ETL action requires the standard plan, preview, explicit approval, and execute flow.
- Never pass secret values in chat, shell arguments, logs, docs, or JSON output.

## Command Surface

- Run 582 source-bound direct reads, four GitLab ETL streams, and 381 source-bound mutations through direct-write and approval-gated reverse-ETL commands.
- Usage: pm gitlab <command> [flags]
- Source CLI: GitLab REST API v4 (Official OpenAPI 3.0.0, info.version 19.3.0-pre, retrieved 2026-08-05)
- PM execution policy pm-request-contract-bounds-v1: each max N bytes qualifier is the effective PM request limit, not a provider schema assertion; path/query values are measured after exact wire encoding and rejected rather than truncated.
- Global flags:
  - --credential (string): Credential name to use for the GitLab request.
  - --connection (string): Alias for --credential.
  - --config (string_array): Connector config override as key=value; never pass secret values here.
  - --json (boolean): Emit machine-readable JSON output.
  - --limit (integer): Maximum records to emit from a stream command.
  - --approval-token-stdin (boolean): Read the approval token as one bounded line from standard input.
- ETL streams
  - projects list - List GitLab projects as ETL records. [intent=etl availability=implemented stream=projects]
  - groups list - List GitLab groups as ETL records. [intent=etl availability=implemented stream=groups]
  - users list - List GitLab users as ETL records. [intent=etl availability=implemented stream=users]
  - issues list - List GitLab issues as ETL records. [intent=etl availability=implemented stream=issues]

- Help topics:
  - authentication - Store GitLab access tokens with pm credentials; never pass secret values in command text.
  - operation-inventory - The GitLab source-lock ledger retains 1,752 cited REST identities: 582 direct reads, four ETL-only stream reads, and 381 mutations with direct-write and approval-gated reverse-ETL commands are executable through closed contracts.
  - known-limits - The remaining 785 source identities are discoverable named-foundation blocked outcomes; 1,845 source-contract gap entries overlap by operation and are not a row denominator.

## Sync Transport

- Source transport: declared
- Destination transport: unsupported
- A declared transport still requires runtime preflight and externally verified conformance; it is not a certification claim.
- Source executor: declarative_api/declarative_stream_source

## Enabled Connector Contract

- Source lock: sources/gitlab-operation-source-lock.json (sha256=b33e80759af3a529d71a0bb58c8c76d65c9f0b20774a042196c6d3c0c57310bd, bytes=3611238).
- Lane states are source-backed contract outcomes; certification and credential availability do not change them.
- binary_download: deferred_foundation (source coverage: partial 0/1; unmapped=0; deferred=1; unsupported=0) — GitLab documents a raw repository-file download, but the retained page does not publish a closed response-media contract or complete executable request/response schema; source_contract_unavailable remains explicit until a source-citable binary response contract is retained.
- binary_upload: deferred_foundation (source coverage: partial 0/1; unmapped=0; deferred=1; unsupported=0) — GitLab documents generic-package file upload, but the retained page does not publish a closed request-media contract or complete typed request/response schema; source_contract_unavailable remains explicit until a source-citable binary upload contract is retained.
- direct_read: implemented (source coverage: partial 582/749; unmapped=0; deferred=167; unsupported=0) — 582 exact source-bound GET direct reads are command-dispatchable; the remaining GET/HEAD identities retain their per-source named foundation outcomes.
- direct_write: implemented (source coverage: partial 381/382; unmapped=0; deferred=1; unsupported=0) — 381 source-bound mutations reuse their closed reverse-ETL write action through a one-request direct-write command; one retained source request-schema row and the remaining mutation identities retain their per-source runtime gaps.
- etl: implemented (source coverage: complete 4/4; unmapped=0; deferred=0; unsupported=0) — Four fixed GET streams use the declared source executor and the local DuckDB/Parquet warehouse destination; no API-to-API route is selected.
- reverse_etl: implemented (source coverage: partial 381/1003; unmapped=0; deferred=622; unsupported=0) — 381 source-bound mutations are available only through DuckDB/Parquet-backed reverse plan and preview before approval-gated dispatch; 622 remaining mutations, including one retained request-schema row, retain named foundation outcomes.
- sync_transport: implemented (source coverage: complete 4/4; unmapped=0; deferred=0; unsupported=0) — The shared declarative source is registered with immutable conformance evidence for a bounded full-snapshot route to the local durable DuckDB/Parquet warehouse; order, cursors, and delete events are not claimed where the locked source does not declare them.

## Commands

### Inspect as a manual

```bash
pm connectors inspect gitlab
```

### Inspect as structured JSON

```bash
pm connectors inspect gitlab --json
```

## Agent Rules

- Run pm connectors inspect gitlab before creating credentials or plans.
- Use --json only when the caller needs structured output; use the manual for human-readable guidance.
- Never ask the user to paste secret values into chat.
- For reverse ETL writes, create a plan, show the preview, wait for explicit approval, then run with the approval token.
